Over the following year, as he continued mentoring junior staff and writing for a senior tech newsletter, these ideas hardened into a fixed conviction that certain coworkers were covertly monitoring his computer projects and trying to sabotage his reputation, even when audits and HR investigations found no irregularities. His late-night over-planning and coffee-fueled troubleshooting gradually centered more on documenting perceived plots than on actual technical problems, creating tension with colleagues and minor performance concerns despite his otherwise intact skills. He finally agreed to see a psychiatrist after his wife, worried by his insistence that the company was secretly building a case to fire only him and his refusal to accept reassurances from supervisors, asked their primary care doctor for a referral.\n- Chief Complaint: “My job says everything is fine, but I know certain people at work are secretly working against me and I can’t be convinced otherwise.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Even though HR and my supervisor keep telling me everything checks out, I’m completely convinced a couple of guys on my team are quietly logging into my tickets, tweaking things to make me look incompetent, and feeding that to management so they can eventually push me out, and no amount of reports or reassurances has shaken that belief.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functioning_not_markedly_impaired_apart_from_delusion: Outside of all this worry about coworkers sabotaging me, I still handle my usual support tickets, mentor the junior staff, and write my regular pieces for the tech newsletter without any trouble.\n- functional_impairment: Because I’m so focused on tracking what I think they’re doing to undermine me, I’ve started spending extra hours documenting every little thing and double-checking work that was already fine, which has slowed me down and led my supervisor to mention slight concerns about my efficiency and tension with a couple of teammates.\n- duration: These ideas about people at work secretly working against me have been going on pretty steadily since the restructuring started a little over a year ago.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: “My job says everything is fine, but I know certain people at work are secretly working against me and I can’t be convinced otherwise.”\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: My job says everything is fine, but I know certain people at work are secretly working against me and I can’t be convinced otherwise. I only agreed to come here because my wife was worried about me, but I know what is really going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed hearing voices or seeing things that other people do not, or is it mainly the belief that people are working against you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices or see things that aren't there at all. It is entirely about what is happening at my job, where I know for a fact that specific coworkers are actively monitoring my projects and trying to sabotage my reputation.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief been the only issue, or have you also had other unusual beliefs, disorganized thinking, or major changes in mood or functioning?\n\nPatient: No, my mind is completely clear and this is the only issue; I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Apart from spending extra hours documenting everything to protect myself, my day-to-day functioning is totally normal—I still handle my support tickets, mentor junior staff, and write for our tech newsletter without any trouble.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers and job, or have you also started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved or that the problem is affecting other areas too?\n\nPatient: No, this is strictly limited to those specific coworkers at my job, and I don't suspect anyone else in my personal life. Outside of work, my daily routines are completely normal and I don't have any issues with friends or neighbors. My wife is only worried because she sees how much stress this work situation is causing me.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers at work, or have you started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved too?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly limited to those couple of guys on my team at work. My home life is completely normal, and my relationship with my wife is fine, though she does worry about how stressed I've been. I don't believe anyone outside of my office is involved in this at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been certain that these coworkers are targeting you—has it been less than 3 months, or 3 months or longer?\n\nPatient: It has been much longer than three months, actually. This has been going on steadily for about fourteen months now, starting right around the time our department went through a restructuring. It's been a constant issue since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods during these 14 months when the belief completely went away, or has it been present continuously the whole time?\n\nPatient: No, it has been present continuously without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring started 14 months ago, I've had to deal with this every single day. There hasn't been a single moment where I felt like the situation had gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or broadcast to others?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like hearing voices, seeing things, or having my thoughts controlled. My mind is completely my own, and the only issue is what those specific people at work are doing to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that the people at work were doing anything impossible or clearly untrue, or does it feel like something that could realistically happen even if others disagree?\n\nPatient: It is completely realistic and actually very easy to pull off. Since I work in IT, I know firsthand how simple it is for someone to log into my tickets and tweak things to make me look incompetent. This isn't some crazy fantasy, but a very practical threat that I am certain is happening, even if others claim they can't find any proof.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other related beliefs or concerns outside of this one work situation, or is it only this single, well-organized belief about your coworkers?"

It is entirely about what is happening at my job, where I know for a fact that specific coworkers are actively monitoring my projects and trying to sabotage my reputation.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief been the only issue, or have you also had other unusual beliefs, disorganized thinking, or major changes in mood or functioning?\n\nPatient: No, my mind is completely clear and this is the only issue; I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Apart from spending extra hours documenting everything to protect myself, my day-to-day functioning is totally normal—I still handle my support tickets, mentor junior staff, and write for our tech newsletter without any trouble.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers and job, or have you also started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved or that the problem is affecting other areas too?\n\nPatient: No, this is strictly limited to those specific coworkers at my job, and I don't suspect anyone else in my personal life. Outside of work, my daily routines are completely normal and I don't have any issues with friends or neighbors. My wife is only worried because she sees how much stress this work situation is causing me.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers at work, or have you started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved too?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly limited to those couple of guys on my team at work. My home life is completely normal, and my relationship with my wife is fine, though she does worry about how stressed I've been. I don't believe anyone outside of my office is involved in this at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been certain that these coworkers are targeting you—has it been less than 3 months, or 3 months or longer?\n\nPatient: It has been much longer than three months, actually. This has been going on steadily for about fourteen months now, starting right around the time our department went through a restructuring. It's been a constant issue since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods during these 14 months when the belief completely went away, or has it been present continuously the whole time?\n\nPatient: No, it has been present continuously without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring started 14 months ago, I've had to deal with this every single day. There hasn't been a single moment where I felt like the situation had gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or broadcast to others?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like hearing voices, seeing things, or having my thoughts controlled. My mind is completely my own, and the only issue is what those specific people at work are doing to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that the people at work were doing anything impossible or clearly untrue, or does it feel like something that could realistically happen even if others disagree?\n\nPatient: It is completely realistic and actually very easy to pull off. Since I work in IT, I know firsthand how simple it is for someone to log into my tickets and tweak things to make me look incompetent. This isn't some crazy fantasy, but a very practical threat that I am certain is happening, even if others claim they can't find any proof.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other related beliefs or concerns outside of this one work situation, or is it only this single, well-organized belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly this single concern about those specific coworkers, and I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Outside of this, my life is completely normal; I still mentor junior staff, write for our tech newsletter, and enjoy my home life without any issues. This work situation is the only thing I am concerned about.\n\nDoctor: During the past 14 months, have you had any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest that lasted for days or weeks while the belief was present?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest at all. My mood has been completely stable, and I still enjoy my usual hobbies and mentoring. My only real concern is just what is happening with those specific people at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or other psychotic symptoms at any point, even briefly, or has it always been only this one belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or anything like that. It has always been just this one clear issue with those specific coworkers sabotaging my projects to get me pushed out. My mind is completely sharp, and this is the only thing I'm dealing with."

Outside of work, my daily routines are completely normal and I don't have any issues with friends or neighbors. My wife is only worried because she sees how much stress this work situation is causing me.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers at work, or have you started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved too?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly limited to those couple of guys on my team at work. My home life is completely normal, and my relationship with my wife is fine, though she does worry about how stressed I've been. I don't believe anyone outside of my office is involved in this at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been certain that these coworkers are targeting you—has it been less than 3 months, or 3 months or longer?\n\nPatient: It has been much longer than three months, actually. This has been going on steadily for about fourteen months now, starting right around the time our department went through a restructuring. It's been a constant issue since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods during these 14 months when the belief completely went away, or has it been present continuously the whole time?\n\nPatient: No, it has been present continuously without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring started 14 months ago, I've had to deal with this every single day. There hasn't been a single moment where I felt like the situation had gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or broadcast to others?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like hearing voices, seeing things, or having my thoughts controlled. My mind is completely my own, and the only issue is what those specific people at work are doing to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that the people at work were doing anything impossible or clearly untrue, or does it feel like something that could realistically happen even if others disagree?\n\nPatient: It is completely realistic and actually very easy to pull off. Since I work in IT, I know firsthand how simple it is for someone to log into my tickets and tweak things to make me look incompetent. This isn't some crazy fantasy, but a very practical threat that I am certain is happening, even if others claim they can't find any proof.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other related beliefs or concerns outside of this one work situation, or is it only this single, well-organized belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly this single concern about those specific coworkers, and I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Outside of this, my life is completely normal; I still mentor junior staff, write for our tech newsletter, and enjoy my home life without any issues. This work situation is the only thing I am concerned about.\n\nDoctor: During the past 14 months, have you had any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest that lasted for days or weeks while the belief was present?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest at all. My mood has been completely stable, and I still enjoy my usual hobbies and mentoring. My only real concern is just what is happening with those specific people at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or other psychotic symptoms at any point, even briefly, or has it always been only this one belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or anything like that. It has always been just this one clear issue with those specific coworkers sabotaging my projects to get me pushed out. My mind is completely sharp, and this is the only thing I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused you to take actions that have led to problems at work or in your relationships, such as confronting coworkers, filing complaints, or changing your behavior in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely changed my behavior because I now spend extra hours obsessively documenting every detail and double-checking work that is already fine to protect myself. This has slowed down my efficiency, which led my supervisor to mention some minor concerns about my productivity. It has also created some noticeable tension between me and a few of my teammates.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief ever led you to accuse, confront, or take formal action against these coworkers, or caused any serious problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: I haven't directly confronted them or filed any formal charges, but I have spent a lot of extra time documenting everything to protect myself. This has caused some tension with my teammates and minor concerns from my supervisor about my efficiency, and my wife became so worried about my constant stress over this plot that she insisted I come here."
  }

There hasn't been a single moment where I felt like the situation had gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or broadcast to others?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like hearing voices, seeing things, or having my thoughts controlled. My mind is completely my own, and the only issue is what those specific people at work are doing to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that the people at work were doing anything impossible or clearly untrue, or does it feel like something that could realistically happen even if others disagree?\n\nPatient: It is completely realistic and actually very easy to pull off. Since I work in IT, I know firsthand how simple it is for someone to log into my tickets and tweak things to make me look incompetent. This isn't some crazy fantasy, but a very practical threat that I am certain is happening, even if others claim they can't find any proof.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other related beliefs or concerns outside of this one work situation, or is it only this single, well-organized belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly this single concern about those specific coworkers, and I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Outside of this, my life is completely normal; I still mentor junior staff, write for our tech newsletter, and enjoy my home life without any issues. This work situation is the only thing I am concerned about.\n\nDoctor: During the past 14 months, have you had any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest that lasted for days or weeks while the belief was present?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest at all. My mood has been completely stable, and I still enjoy my usual hobbies and mentoring. My only real concern is just what is happening with those specific people at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or other psychotic symptoms at any point, even briefly, or has it always been only this one belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or anything like that. It has always been just this one clear issue with those specific coworkers sabotaging my projects to get me pushed out. My mind is completely sharp, and this is the only thing I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused you to take actions that have led to problems at work or in your relationships, such as confronting coworkers, filing complaints, or changing your behavior in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely changed my behavior because I now spend extra hours obsessively documenting every detail and double-checking work that is already fine to protect myself. This has slowed down my efficiency, which led my supervisor to mention some minor concerns about my productivity. It has also created some noticeable tension between me and a few of my teammates.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief ever led you to accuse, confront, or take formal action against these coworkers, or caused any serious problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: I haven't directly confronted them or filed any formal charges, but I have spent a lot of extra time documenting everything to protect myself. This has caused some tension with my teammates and minor concerns from my supervisor about my efficiency, and my wife became so worried about my constant stress over this plot that she insisted I come here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are only spying on and sabotaging you, or do you also think they are trying to harm you in some other way, such as poisoning you, stealing from you, or causing physical danger?\n\nPatient: No, they aren't trying to poison me, steal from me, or physically harm me in any way. Their actions are strictly focused on sabotaging my professional reputation, messing with my computer tickets, and trying to get me fired. This is entirely about undermining my work, not causing me physical danger."

It's been a constant issue since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods during these 14 months when the belief completely went away, or has it been present continuously the whole time?\n\nPatient: No, it has been present continuously without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring started 14 months ago, I've had to deal with this every single day. There hasn't been a single moment where I felt like the situation had gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or broadcast to others?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like hearing voices, seeing things, or having my thoughts controlled. My mind is completely my own, and the only issue is what those specific people at work are doing to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that the people at work were doing anything impossible or clearly untrue, or does it feel like something that could realistically happen even if others disagree?\n\nPatient: It is completely realistic and actually very easy to pull off. Since I work in IT, I know firsthand how simple it is for someone to log into my tickets and tweak things to make me look incompetent. This isn't some crazy fantasy, but a very practical threat that I am certain is happening, even if others claim they can't find any proof.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other related beliefs or concerns outside of this one work situation, or is it only this single, well-organized belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly this single concern about those specific coworkers, and I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Outside of this, my life is completely normal; I still mentor junior staff, write for our tech newsletter, and enjoy my home life without any issues. This work situation is the only thing I am concerned about.\n\nDoctor: During the past 14 months, have you had any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest that lasted for days or weeks while the belief was present?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest at all. My mood has been completely stable, and I still enjoy my usual hobbies and mentoring. My only real concern is just what is happening with those specific people at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or other psychotic symptoms at any point, even briefly, or has it always been only this one belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or anything like that. It has always been just this one clear issue with those specific coworkers sabotaging my projects to get me pushed out. My mind is completely sharp, and this is the only thing I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused you to take actions that have led to problems at work or in your relationships, such as confronting coworkers, filing complaints, or changing your behavior in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely changed my behavior because I now spend extra hours obsessively documenting every detail and double-checking work that is already fine to protect myself. This has slowed down my efficiency, which led my supervisor to mention some minor concerns about my productivity. It has also created some noticeable tension between me and a few of my teammates.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief ever led you to accuse, confront, or take formal action against these coworkers, or caused any serious problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: I haven't directly confronted them or filed any formal charges, but I have spent a lot of extra time documenting everything to protect myself. This has caused some tension with my teammates and minor concerns from my supervisor about my efficiency, and my wife became so worried about my constant stress over this plot that she insisted I come here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are only spying on and sabotaging you, or do you also think they are trying to harm you in some other way, such as poisoning you, stealing from you, or causing physical danger?\n\nPatient: No, they aren't trying to poison me, steal from me, or physically harm me in any way. Their actions are strictly focused on sabotaging my professional reputation, messing with my computer tickets, and trying to get me fired. This is entirely about undermining my work, not causing me physical danger.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused a clear drop in your work performance or made it hard for you to keep up with your usual responsibilities? Or are you still functioning about the same as before aside from the extra stress and checking?\n\nPatient: I'm still functioning quite well in my core duties, like handling support tickets, mentoring junior staff, and writing for the tech newsletter. However, because I have to spend so much extra time documenting and double-checking everything to protect myself, it has slowed me down a bit. My supervisor has mentioned some minor concerns about my efficiency, and there is definitely some tension with a couple of my teammates."

I only agreed to come here because my wife was worried about me, but I know what is really going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed hearing voices or seeing things that other people do not, or is it mainly the belief that people are working against you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices or see things that aren't there at all. It is entirely about what is happening at my job, where I know for a fact that specific coworkers are actively monitoring my projects and trying to sabotage my reputation.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief been the only issue, or have you also had other unusual beliefs, disorganized thinking, or major changes in mood or functioning?\n\nPatient: No, my mind is completely clear and this is the only issue; I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Apart from spending extra hours documenting everything to protect myself, my day-to-day functioning is totally normal—I still handle my support tickets, mentor junior staff, and write for our tech newsletter without any trouble.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers and job, or have you also started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved or that the problem is affecting other areas too?\n\nPatient: No, this is strictly limited to those specific coworkers at my job, and I don't suspect anyone else in my personal life. Outside of work, my daily routines are completely normal and I don't have any issues with friends or neighbors. My wife is only worried because she sees how much stress this work situation is causing me.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief stayed limited to your coworkers at work, or have you started believing that people in other parts of your life are involved too?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly limited to those couple of guys on my team at work. My home life is completely normal, and my relationship with my wife is fine, though she does worry about how stressed I've been. I don't believe anyone outside of my office is involved in this at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been certain that these coworkers are targeting you—has it been less than 3 months, or 3 months or longer?\n\nPatient: It has been much longer than three months, actually. This has been going on steadily for about fourteen months now, starting right around the time our department went through a restructuring. It's been a constant issue since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods during these 14 months when the belief completely went away, or has it been present continuously the whole time?\n\nPatient: No, it has been present continuously without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring started 14 months ago, I've had to deal with this every single day. There hasn't been a single moment where I felt like the situation had gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or broadcast to others?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like hearing voices, seeing things, or having my thoughts controlled. My mind is completely my own, and the only issue is what those specific people at work are doing to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that the people at work were doing anything impossible or clearly untrue, or does it feel like something that could realistically happen even if others disagree?\n\nPatient: It is completely realistic and actually very easy to pull off. Since I work in IT, I know firsthand how simple it is for someone to log into my tickets and tweak things to make me look incompetent. This isn't some crazy fantasy, but a very practical threat that I am certain is happening, even if others claim they can't find any proof.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other related beliefs or concerns outside of this one work situation, or is it only this single, well-organized belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, it is strictly this single concern about those specific coworkers, and I don't have any other unusual beliefs. Outside of this, my life is completely normal; I still mentor junior staff, write for our tech newsletter, and enjoy my home life without any issues. This work situation is the only thing I am concerned about.\n\nDoctor: During the past 14 months, have you had any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest that lasted for days or weeks while the belief was present?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any periods of depression, elevated mood, or loss of interest at all. My mood has been completely stable, and I still enjoy my usual hobbies and mentoring. My only real concern is just what is happening with those specific people at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or other psychotic symptoms at any point, even briefly, or has it always been only this one belief about your coworkers?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any hallucinations, disorganized thoughts, or anything like that. It has always been just this one clear issue with those specific coworkers sabotaging my projects to get me pushed out. My mind is completely sharp, and this is the only thing I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused you to take actions that have led to problems at work or in your relationships, such as confronting coworkers, filing complaints, or changing your behavior in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely changed my behavior because I now spend extra hours obsessively documenting every detail and double-checking work that is already fine to protect myself. This has slowed down my efficiency, which led my supervisor to mention some minor concerns about my productivity. It has also created some noticeable tension between me and a few of my teammates.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief ever led you to accuse, confront, or take formal action against these coworkers, or caused any serious problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: I haven't directly confronted them or filed any formal charges, but I have spent a lot of extra time documenting everything to protect myself. This has caused some tension with my teammates and minor concerns from my supervisor about my efficiency, and my wife became so worried about my constant stress over this plot that she insisted I come here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are only spying on and sabotaging you, or do you also think they are trying to harm you in some other way, such as poisoning you, stealing from you, or causing physical danger?\n\nPatient: No, they aren't trying to poison me, steal from me, or physically harm me in any way. Their actions are strictly focused on sabotaging my professional reputation, messing with my computer tickets, and trying to get me fired. This is entirely about undermining my work, not causing me physical danger.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused a clear drop in your work performance or made it hard for you to keep up with your usual responsibilities? Or are you still functioning about the same as before aside from the extra stress and checking?\n\nPatient: I'm still functioning quite well in my core duties, like handling support tickets, mentoring junior staff, and writing for the tech newsletter. However, because I have to spend so much extra time documenting and double-checking everything to protect myself, it has slowed me down a bit. My supervisor has mentioned some minor concerns about my efficiency, and there is definitely some tension with a couple of my teammates.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the extra time you spend documenting and checking things, has this belief caused a clear drop in your overall work performance or daily functioning? Or are you still basically able to do your job and normal activities about as well as before?\n\nPatient: Yes, my overall ability to do my job and go about my daily life is completely intact. Aside from the extra hours I spend double-checking things to protect myself, I am still fully capable of handling my usual support tickets, mentoring the junior staff, and writing my pieces for the tech newsletter. Everything else in my life is running just as smoothly as before."
